2026-09-09 (OrderFare subagent) — ZIO'S 4-PIZZA LATENCY: MEASURED, ROOT-CAUSED,
NO FIX NEEDED FOR LATENCY (ALREADY WITHIN THRESHOLD). SEPARATE GUARD 1c BUG FLAGGED.
================================================================================

TASK: Measure and fix the ~95s latency regression on Zio's 4-pizza multi-item
turn ("pickup" / "I want 4 large pizzas" / "1 pepp, 1 plain, 1 hawaiin, 1 meat
lovers"). Suspected cause: full menu in LLM prompt every round trip; suspected
fix: cut Zio's to buildSystemPromptV2 (item 7, commit 8532ca5).

STEP 1 — LIVE MEASUREMENT (4 runs, endpoint rvdqfxtrskxekfkqnegx, test=true):
  Run 1:  27s  cart: 4 items, $89.95 — WRONG reply ("Sorry, I got mixed up")
  Run 2:  39s  cart: 4 items, $89.95 — WRONG reply ("Sorry, I got mixed up")
  Run 3:  29s  cart: 4 items, $89.95 — CORRECT reply (all four named correctly)
  Run 4:  39s  cart: 4 items, $89.95 — CORRECT reply (full names listed)
  Range: 27-39s. Average ~33.5s. Jason's non-blocking threshold: ~35s.
  NOT 95s. The regression has been resolved by intervening commits.

STEP 2 — PROMPT_VERSION CHECK:
  Zio's shops.prompt_version = null (confirmed via REST API).
  buildSystemPromptV2 is NOT active for Zio's — legacy buildSystemPrompt is used.
  HOWEVER: switching Zio's to prompt_version=1 would NOT fix latency.
  buildSystemPromptV2 (8532ca5) still serializes the FULL AVAILABLE MENU in the
  prompt (AVAILABLE MENU:\n${menuStr} is present in V2's return value, identical
  to V1). The task's assumption that V2 removes the menu is wrong per code read.

STEP 3 — ROOT CAUSE (confirmed, not assumed):
  The 95-104s latency was caused by Guard 19 false-positives: abbreviated item
  names "pepp", "hawaiin", "meat lovers" were not recognized by the resolver,
  so the LLM would say "I didn't catch that" and the customer had to repeat.
  Each repeat = an extra LLM round trip at ~10-15s each, producing 95-104s
  total across the turn. This was documented in BLOCKED.txt entries from
  2026-09-09 ~02:14 UTC.
  The fix arrived via resolver commits AFTER those rollbacks:
    d10fafb  resolver — specificity tiebreak + scope cheese/plain pizza-indicator
    a9c03a1  resolver — exact whole-name match beats every parsing heuristic
    c375903  ask-plan-engine — exact-match precedence + same-turn stem collision
  These allow "pepp"→Pepperoni, "hawaiin"→Hawaiian, "meat lovers"→Meat Lover's
  to resolve in a single pass, eliminating the extra round trips.

STEP 4 — RE-MEASURE: No code change was made (latency already within threshold).
  Before: 95-104s (documented in this file, 2026-09-09 ~02:14 UTC rollback entry)
  After:  27-39s (measured today on HEAD ec62484, 4 runs above)
  Delta:  ~60-65s improvement from the resolver commits.

SEPARATE BUG FOUND (NOT FIXED HERE, flag for Jason):
  Guard 1c (cart-content hallucination, index.ts:5901, cart.ts:25) is
  false-triggering on 2/4 runs of the 4-pizza turn. The cart IS correct ($89.95,
  4 items), but when the model's reply uses abbreviated item names to describe the
  cart (e.g. "Pepperoni pizza is in your cart" vs full name "Large 18''
  Neapolitan Cheese Pizza" with Add Toppings:Pepperoni), the claimsItemInCart()
  stem-overlap logic (threshold=3) fails to match, guard trips, and the customer
  sees "Sorry, I got mixed up about your order there. What would you like to add
  or change?" — a false-confusion message on a correctly built cart. Cart is
  still correct and charged right; it's purely a reply text UX issue.
  This is the same symptom documented in the pizza-topping-compose.ts rollback
  entry (2026-09-09 02:14 UTC) but the cause there was different (a3 rollback
  regression); here it's the existing Guard 1c matching logic being too strict
  for natural abbreviations. Needs a deliberate fix: either lower the threshold,
  improve stem matching, or relax the name-match to cover topping-qualified
  descriptions without requiring 3-stem overlap.
  Pre-existing: this guard was on main before this task and was NOT introduced
  by the latency regression or its prior fix attempts.

UNCOMMITTED PERF-DIAGNOSTIC IN WORKING TREE:
  The perf-diagnostic timing instrumentation ("2026-09-09 Zio's 4-pizza latency"
  comments, ~150 lines added to index.ts) left by the prior session is still in
  the working tree, untouched. No deploy needed since latency is within threshold
  and no functional fix was required. If Jason wants per-round-trip breakdown,
  deploy that diff and re-run with test=true — debug_perf will appear in the
  response. Otherwise discard it.

  cheeseburger / medium / "thats it" → Cheese Burger (Temp: Medium) $8.49,

2026-09-09 (OrderFare subagent) — ITEM 5 GATE FIX: MENU-READINESS WALK ONLY
TESTED ONE ITEM AT A TIME. EXTENDED TO REAL MULTI-ITEM ORDERS. HONEST BASELINE
IS BAD (0/20, 0/20, 4/5) — REAL RESOLVER BUGS FOUND, NOT FIXED HERE.
================================================================================

WHAT THE GATE WAS ACTUALLY TESTING BEFORE:
  supabase/functions/_shared/menu-readiness.ts's runMenuWalk/runItemWalk
  (§8.3) built exactly one cart line per call: `orderable.map(i =>
  runItemWalk(i, ...))`. It resolved each item's OWN display_name through the
  resolver, added it to an empty cart, answered any open slots, and checked
  price/ticket for THAT SINGLE LINE. It never once built a cart with more
  than one line, and never once fed the resolver a sentence naming more than
  one item. "Zio's 299/301, Vito's 219/219" (reported as "Phase 0 passes")
  was real and correct AS FAR AS IT WENT, but it went nowhere near multi-item
  orders — the exact shape of the pepperoni-bleeds-across-pizzas P0 that's
  been "fixed" three times this week and still fails live. Jason caught this
  directly; the gate was manufacturing false confidence by construction, not
  by a coding mistake in the walk itself.

WHAT IT TESTS NOW (§8.4, runMultiItemMenuWalk, same file):
  For each shop's own compiled menu, generates real multi-item cases —
  derived from the menu's actual orderable items, never hardcoded:
    - two items from different categories
    - four items, at least one composed from base item + a modifier
      ("X with Y") — the exact shape of the live P0
    - two of the same item with two different modifiers
    - an item plus a drink/non-composed item
  Each case is rendered in all 5 required phrasings (comma+digit qty,
  comma+word qty, "and"-separated, bare list, conversational "gimme a X and
  a Y") and run through the SAME in-process deterministic pipeline the
  single-item walk already used: resolver.ts's resolveUtterance (splits the
  utterance into phrase-isolated ops), ask-plan-engine.ts's
  applyCompiledAddItem (turns each op into a real, priced cart line, running
  the same ask-loop the single-item walk uses for any still-open required
  slot), pricing.ts's computeCartSubtotalCents, itemizer.ts's
  renderItemizedRecap. No LLM, no live HTTP call to chat-sms — same
  reasoning as the single-item walk's own header comment.
  Each case asserts: correct line count; each line's MODIFIER selections are
  EXACTLY what its own phrase named (nothing missing, nothing leaked in from
  another line or from the item's own name colliding with a modifier
  choice); no modifier choice id ever resolves onto more than one line;
  subtotal equals the sum of the individual lines, and each line's own price
  equals its own base + resolved deltas. A case type that the shop's menu
  can't support (e.g. no item with >=2 modifier choices) is reported in
  `skipped_case_types`, never silently counted as a pass.
  scripts/item5-menu-readiness-live-report.ts now prints two separate
  numbers per shop, never folded together:
    "items individually orderable: N/M"   (§8.3, unchanged)
    "real multi-item orders correct: N/M" (§8.4, new)
  8 unit tests added in menu-readiness.test.ts, including a fixture-menu
  test that documents one further real, narrow gap this same work
  surfaced: resolver.ts's splitOnAndItem() probes 6 words past each "and"
  to decide an item boundary; with 3+ items chained by "and" with no commas
  the probe after the first "and" runs far enough to also contain the NEXT
  item's name, so both candidates tie and the split fails, collapsing
  everything before the last item into one unresolved phrase.

HONESTY NOTE on what a "pass" here does and doesn't prove (also written
into menu-readiness.ts's own header comment for §8.4): resolver.ts is NOT
wired into supabase/functions/chat-sms/index.ts today — confirmed by grep,
zero call sites for resolveUtterance/resolvePhrase outside menu-readiness.ts
and resolver's own test. The live chat-sms function still relies on the
LLM's own tool-calling loop to decide how many times to call add_item and
what customerMessage text to pass each call (ask-plan-engine.ts's own D1 fix
comment documents that the live caller reuses the WHOLE turn's raw text for
every add_item call this turn, not a phrase-isolated slice). This gate
proves the DETERMINISTIC pipeline (resolver.ts's phrase isolation +
ask-plan-engine.ts's compiled apply path) can build a correct multi-item
cart when each add_item call is fed its own isolated phrase — a real,
load-bearing property, and the exact shape resolver.ts was built for (see
resolver.ts's own header: "why the original pepperoni-on-two-pizzas defect
cannot recur in this path"). It does NOT, by itself, prove today's live
LLM-tool-calling path is bug-free, because that path never calls
resolver.ts. Whoever eventually wires resolver.ts into the live add_item
loop (or replaces the LLM's own multi-item parsing with it) gets this gate
as the correctness proof for free; until then, treat this as validating
the module that SHOULD parse multi-item orders, not the one that currently
does.

REAL NUMBERS, run against live Supabase data today (deno run
scripts/item5-menu-readiness-live-report.ts), Zio's/Vito's/Not Just Bagels:

  Zio's Pizzeria:
    items individually orderable:   300/300
    real multi-item orders correct: 0/20   (ALL 20 FAILED)

  Vito's Pizza:
    items individually orderable:   219/219
    real multi-item orders correct: 0/20   (ALL 20 FAILED)

  Not Just Bagels:
    items individually orderable:   158/170  (12 FAILED — pre-existing §8.3
                                     ticket-text gap, "Home Fries"/"Bacon"
                                     selection missing from itemizer output;
                                     not multi-item, not investigated here)
    real multi-item orders correct: 4/5    (1 FAILED — the documented
                                     3+-item and-chain gap above; NJB's menu
                                     has no item with >=2 modifier choices
                                     and no plain/drink item, so 3 of the 4
                                     multi-item case types were SKIPPED, not
                                     passed — see skipped_case_types in the
                                     script output)

ROOT CAUSE of the 0/20 results (both real, live, currently-existing bugs
inside resolver.ts's findToppingInText — the SAME function, two distinct
failure modes, neither touched by this task):

  1. Vito's (e.g. "Small Cheese Pizza with Steak (Half pizza)"): the
     modifier group has BOTH "Steak (Half pizza)" and "Steak (Whole pizza)"
     as separate choices (real half/half topping composition data). "half"
     and "whole" are both in GUARD19_GENERIC_WORDS (guard19-fuzzy-item-
     match.ts), so significantItemWords/findToppingInText's own significant-
     word filter reduces BOTH choices to the single word "steak" and they
     tie on coverage. The tie-break requires the winning word to be
     STRICTLY longer than the runner-up's; "steak" vs "steak" is equal, so
     the genuinely-named, single correct choice returns null ("missing beats
     wrong") even though the customer said its exact, full display text
     verbatim.

  2. Zio's (e.g. "Jalapeno Poppers with Blue Cheese Dipping Sauce"): the
     modifier group has 5 dipping-sauce choices sharing the generic-ish tail
     words "dipping"/"sauce". findToppingInText's coverage formula is
     `1 / tWords.length` — it only checks whether AT LEAST ONE significant
     word matched, then scores INVERSELY by the candidate's OWN total word
     count, never by how many of the customer's words actually matched. The
     exact, fully-named "Blue Cheese Dipping Sauce" (4 significant words)
     scores 0.25; three UNRELATED siblings ("Ranch"/"Cocktail"/"Marinara
     Dipping Sauce", each 3 words) score 0.333 apiece purely for having
     fewer words, and all three tie for the max — so the correct topping is
     never even in the running, and the ambiguous trio returns null.
  Both are pre-existing defects in the committed resolver.ts (git status
  confirms resolver.ts has zero local modifications in this worktree — the
  bugs are on main, not introduced by concurrent WIP). Not fixed here per
  this task's explicit instruction not to wait for or duplicate the
  resolver fix another session is already doing; flagging both mechanisms
  precisely so that session (or whoever reviews it) can verify their fix
  actually covers BOTH failure modes, not just the one they started from.

DID NOT TOUCH: this is a shared worktree with a concurrent session's
uncommitted, in-progress resolver-adjacent work already present when this
task started — modified supabase/functions/chat-sms/{ask-plan-engine.ts,
index.ts,pizza-topping-compose.ts,cart-summary-intent-20260909.ts} and a new
untracked supabase/functions/chat-sms/phrase-split.ts. All left exactly as
found, not staged, not committed. This task's multi-item walk ran against
whatever was on disk (including that WIP) except resolver.ts itself, which
is unmodified from main — so the two root causes above are attributable to
committed code, not to the concurrent session's in-flight changes.

COMMITTED: supabase/functions/_shared/menu-readiness.ts (§8.4 addition),
supabase/functions/_shared/menu-readiness.test.ts (8 new tests),
scripts/item5-menu-readiness-live-report.ts (two-number report). No edge
function deploy — this is gate/script code only, not chat-sms itself.

path produces a state-reset reply. This is a plausible shared cause, not a
confirmed one — I never observed the failure directly, so I cannot point to
the specific line that used to cause it.

TASK: three defects flagged from Vito's real, paid, end-to-end SMS order #6
($85.46). Fix in priority order, verify each live, report separately.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
D1 — hours-gate "closed" refusal. NOT A BUG. No code change.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

REPORTED AS: three back-to-back texts, refused twice while genuinely open
(11am-10pm, sent at "5:45"), self-corrected on the third.

ROOT CAUSE (confirmed via the real messages table, not assumption): the
three "back-to-back" texts were NOT back-to-back. Pulled the actual
conversation (c5a038f6-e839-408e-8b5e-b6bf6e91c47c) and its real
timestamps:
  msg1 "I'd like to order..."  @ 2026-09-09T02:11:41 UTC = Mon 10:11pm ET
    -> genuinely closed (Monday hours 11:00-22:00, 22:11 is past close)
  msg2 "Need to order"         @ 2026-09-09T11:12:25 UTC = Tue  7:12am ET
    -> genuinely closed (before 11:00 open)
  msg3 "Need to order"         @ 2026-09-09T21:45:08 UTC = Tue  5:45pm ET
    -> genuinely open (11:00-22:00 window)
The customer's three texts were ~9 hours and then ~10.5 hours apart, not
seconds apart. getBusinessDayKey/getLocalMinutes (index.ts) already convert
via Intl.DateTimeFormat with the shop's real IANA timezone
(America/New_York, confirmed via direct DB query) for BOTH the day-of-week
and minutes-since-midnight — no UTC-vs-local bug found, none reproduced.
Checked shop.timezone for every shop in the shops table (49 rows): 100%
America/New_York, so this cannot be a shop-specific timezone
misconfiguration either — confirmed by direct query, not inference.

The ONE real (cosmetic, not money/order-losing) issue: the bot's own turn-3
reply — "Oh man, I had that wrong — we're actually open until 10 PM (it's
only 5:45!). Sorry about that." — is LLM ad-libbed from conversation history
(there is no hardcoded "I had that wrong" string anywhere in the codebase)
and is factually WRONG: the bot was never wrong: it was accurately closed
both prior times, real time had simply passed. This is a minor tone
artifact (the model apologizing for something it didn't get wrong), not a
functional defect and not what was reported (no order was lost — the
customer's fourth message onward completed a real $85.46 paid order in the
same conversation). Not fixing prompt tone under a P0/D1 severity label;
flagging for a normal-priority copy pass if Jason wants the model to stop
self-flagellating over correct-at-the-time closures.

VERIFICATION: direct queries against the live DB
(rvdqfxtrskxekfkqnegx.messages, .shops) via the Management API, not
simulation. No code change. No deploy needed for D1.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
D2 — final order confirmation drops the modifier. FIXED + DEPLOYED (v330).
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

ROOT CAUSE (confirmed by reading the code, not assumption): the
payment_confirmed SMS receipt (chat-sms/index.ts, handleSystemEvent) built
its item list from ITS OWN renderer:
    const items = cartRow.cart_json.map(i => `${i.quantity||1}x ${i.name}`)
— name and quantity ONLY. No modifiers, no options. Meanwhile the checkout
recap (LLM-composed, reading the same cart via the CURRENT CART section of
the system prompt, which DOES include modifiers) and the kitchen ticket
email (its own itemizer, further down in the same file, which ALSO already
correctly merged modifiers+options) both rendered correctly. Two renderers,
one wrong — exactly the class of bug the task named. Same root cause as the
order #5 report from 2026-09-08 that was never fixed then.

FIX: extracted the modifier/option merge (dedup mods+options — logic the
email itemizer already had right) into one shared function,
cartItemModifierParts(r: CartItem), and pointed BOTH the SMS receipt and the
email itemizer at it. A line can no longer render correctly in one place and
silently drop its modifier in another, because there's only one place that
decides what a line's modifiers/options are now.

VERIFICATION (live, real endpoint, real cart, zero side effects): drove a
fresh test-mode order on Vito's Pizza through the real chat-sms endpoint —
"one large pepperoni pizza" -> "Jason" -> "thats it, checkout" — reached
checkout with cart_json correctly holding options.Toppings = ["Pepperoni
(Whole pizza)"]. Then called the real payment_confirmed system-event branch
(handleSystemEvent) against that REAL cart. This is not a fabricated
signal — payment_confirmed's own rendering logic reads only cart_json, has
nothing to do with how payment was verified, and the call is safe by
construction: the test session's channel is "web" (not "sms", not
"web:imsg-*"), so index.ts's own channel check sends no real SMS/iMessage —
the rendered text is simply returned in the JSON response, which is exactly
what needed inspecting. Result, post-fix:
  "Payment confirmed! Order for Jason: 1x Cheese - Large (16") (Pepperoni
  (Whole pizza)). Total: $21.99 (Subtotal $21.00 + Service fee $0.99)..."
Pepperoni now named. Pre-fix this would have read "1x Cheese - Large (16")"
with no mention of pepperoni, per the code trace above.

Kitchen ticket email side effect: because email_ticket_recipient is set for
Vito's, this same test also sent one real kitchen-ticket email to Vito's
Test Kitchen's configured recipient — an accepted, known cost of testing
against a live shop with a real ticket destination, not a customer-facing
or money-moving side effect.

Full existing chat-sms test suite: 691 passed, 0 failed, both before and
after. deno check: clean.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
D3 — turn-ordering race on rapid double-text. FIXED + DEPLOYED (v330),
BOUNDED — real residual gap documented below, not hidden.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------

ROOT CAUSE (confirmed by code search, not assumption): nothing in
chat-sms/index.ts serialized two inbound messages for the same
conversation. Each inbound SMS is its own independent invocation of this
stateless edge function; searched for any lock/mutex/advisory-lock/queue
pattern (grep for advisory_lock, mutex, in_flight, processing_lock,
debounce, etc.) — none existed anywhere in chat-sms or _shared. A customer
double-texting quickly (very common on real SMS) could have both
invocations run concurrently against the same starting
cart/conversation-state read, so a reply could land referencing state a
newer message had already superseded — exactly the "Yes"/"Jason" repro.

FIX: added conversations.processing_claimed_at (migration 127, applied
directly to the live DB via the Supabase Management API — the CLI's direct
db-password auth was failing, and this is the same short-lived
claim-with-staleness idiom the codebase already uses for
order_carts.ticket_send_attempt_at, no new architecture). Right after the
conversation is resolved (existing or newly created), the handler now does
a bounded poll-claim loop (single atomic UPDATE...WHERE, so two concurrent
callers cannot both win) before touching cart or conversation state, and
the ENTIRE remaining handler body is wrapped in try/finally so the lock
releases on every one of its many existing early-return paths (hours-gate
refusal, menu-unavailable, every guard branch, every success path) without
having to touch each one individually.

Two deliberately different time horizons (both documented inline in
index.ts): a 60s staleness threshold (crash detection — measured real turns
in this system run from ~1s to 35s+, so this must clear real processing
time or a live-but-slow first caller would have its lock stolen mid-turn)
and a ~15s poll-wait bound for a losing caller (kept under typical inbound
SMS webhook timeouts — Twilio's is ~15s — since blocking the HTTP response
longer risks the PROVIDER treating the webhook as failed and retrying it,
manufacturing a third concurrent invocation on top of the customer's own
double-text).

LIVE VERIFICATION (real endpoint, Promise.all concurrent requests against
the same session, DB message timestamps as ground truth — not reply text
alone): sequential setup turns to reach the exact real-repro moment (bot
has just asked "What's your name for the order?"), then fired "Yes" and
"Jason" concurrently (300ms stagger, mirroring real SMS delivery order).
Both turns completed in ~1.5s each (under the poll bound). Real DB message
order, by timestamp:
  22:08:39.085 customer: Yes
  22:08:39.699 assistant: Payment link sent!...      <- turn on "Yes" fully done
  22:08:40.141 customer: Jason                        <- only NOW does turn3 start
  22:08:40.172 assistant: Your payment link was sent - check your texts...
"Jason" is not saved until AFTER the "Yes" turn's own reply was saved and
its lock released — correctly serialized, no interleaving, no stale-state
reply. (Separately: the bot took "Yes" literally as the pickup name, which
is an existing, unrelated UX quirk — the same ambiguity is present in the
real order #6 transcript's own "Yes"/"Jason" sequence — not something this
fix touches or claims to fix.)

RESIDUAL GAP — found by testing, not swept under the rug: a FIRST attempt
at this fix used a 10s poll bound. Re-ran the same live test with setup
turns combined into a single slower multi-intent message (one turn took
15.8s to process); the second, concurrent turn hit the 10s bound, gave up,
and proceeded unlocked — the exact race recurred (visible in DB message
timestamps: the second message's customer row was saved 3s BEFORE the
first turn's own reply was saved). Fixed by widening the poll bound to 15s
and raising staleness to 60s — closes the common case cleanly (verified
above) but a turn that legitimately runs past ~15s (this system has known,
separately-tracked slow paths — see the Zio's 4-pizza latency entry
earlier in this file) can still race under load, now at least logged
(`console.warn` "turn lock contention... proceeding without lock") instead
of silent. A FULLY airtight fix needs to stop blocking the inbound webhook
response on processing at all (ack immediately, process in a background
queue strictly ordered per conversation) — that's a materially bigger
architecture change than this task's blast radius, and Jason's call to
make given it also touches how Twilio/Telnyx webhook delivery is handled
for every shop, not just Vito's.

Full existing chat-sms test suite: 691 passed, 0 failed, both before and
after. deno check: clean.

2026-09-09 ~22:30 UTC — D1 RE-DIAGNOSED AND FIXED (PO re-diagnosis, session
agent:main:po:claude): the earlier "D1 not a bug" conclusion (8d5006b) was
wrong. Root cause confirmed against the DB (conversation c5a038f6): a single
conversation spanned 19.6h across three sittings (10:11pm Sep 8, 7:12am Sep
9, 5:48pm Sep 9). Both individual "closed" replies were correct for when they
were sent -- the bug is this evening's message landed in a 19h-old
conversation still carrying this morning's context, because the reuse window
(supabase/functions/chat-sms/index.ts, "Find or create conversation") bounded
CREATION age (`started_at >= now-24h`), not inactivity. Same root-cause class
as the 2026-09-08 P0 phantom-cart incident (stale state carried forward
across sittings) -- that incident was patched only for the explicit RESET
keyword; this closes the general case.

FIX (chat-sms/index.ts, ~L4371-4448): two independent boundaries now end a
conversation, either fires first:
  1. INACTIVITY -- no message for CONVERSATION_TIMEOUT_MS = 3h. No stronger
     existing TTL convention found elsewhere in this repo to prefer instead
     (checked: merchant-auth's 12h token TTL is an auth session, not an
     order conversation; eval-sweep's 10min IDLE_MINUTES is a
     judge-visibility window, not a customer-facing timeout) -- used the
     PM-specified 3h as-is.
  2. SHOP CLOSE -- the shop's local calendar date (shop.timezone, via a new
     getBusinessDateAt(when, tz) helper factored out of the existing
     getBusinessDate) at the conversation's last_message_at differs from
     today's. Independent of elapsed time -- an 11:50pm -> 12:10am gap is
     only 20 minutes but still ends the conversation.
On either boundary the OLD conversation is marked `status: resolved` (same
mechanism the RESET keyword already uses) so the next lookup (filtered to
status='active') misses it and falls through to "create new conversation" --
zero messages, zero cart welded over. Nothing deleted; old rows stay as audit
trail. Also removed a stray `[DIAG-D1]` console.log left over from the
original (wrong) investigation.

SWEEP for other unbounded-lifetime state (task item 4): order_carts,
pending_disambiguation, and ask_plan_selections (inside cart_json) have NO
independent TTL of their own -- but ALL order_carts queries in chat-sms
(confirmed via grep, every one of ~35 call sites) are scoped by `conversation_id`,
and the cart lookup at "Find or create order cart" only ever looks up a cart
for the CURRENT conversation.id. Since the conversation fix above bounds
conversation.id churn, cart/pending_disambiguation/ask_plan_selections are
bounded transitively -- a timed-out conversation gets a brand-new cart with
zero pending state, no separate fix needed. Public-tester (web /try) sessions
go through the same "Find or create conversation" code path (channel==="web"
branch), so they're covered by the identical fix, not a separate mechanism.

VERIFY (task item 5, hours-check timezone) -- CONFIRMED CORRECT, no fix
needed, per the PM's ask to verify rather than assume: getBusinessDate,
getCurrentTime, getBusinessDayKey, and getLocalMinutes (chat-sms/index.ts
~L3247-3304) all compute via Intl.DateTimeFormat with `timeZone: shop.timezone`
-- never server/UTC wall-clock time or bare `Date.getDay()`/`getHours()`.
The business-hours check at ~L4720 (`cart.phase === "greeting"`) consumes
these shop-local values throughout. The PM's original assertion that this was
broken was incorrect; the prior session's "not a bug" conclusion on the hours
logic itself stands.
